### Ereignis 2: Runde 1

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-17ee7c11b9d2`
- Testdateien: `tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs`, `tests/mc-result-cockpit.test.mjs`
- Prüfdimensionen: dimensions checked — DOM restructuring integrity (no duplicate IDs/dead markup), idempotent module initialization, lifecycle state-mirroring correctness (disable/busy sync, delegated single-start), success-only disclosure collapse, XSS/injection surface, failure-path null-guards, and test coverage breadth (unit + keyboard + browser lifecycle)
- Größtes Restrisiko: largest residual risk: reliance on &#96;MutationObserver&#96; as the sole safety net for state mirroring in any future code path that mutates &#96;#mcButton&#96; attributes without going through the existing lifecycle methods
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: break condition: a future slice adds a new path that disables/enables &#96;#mcButton&#96; outside &#96;disableStart/enableStart/beginRun/beginCancelling/finishRun&#96; and without triggering an attribute mutation observable by &#96;MutationObserver&#96; (e.g., a different mechanism than &#96;disabled&#96;/&#96;aria-busy&#96;), causing &#96;#mcRecalculateButton&#96; to desync from the canonical start control.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`

(4) XSS/escaping — all user-controlled highlight/label/error strings route through escapeHtml, comparison error path cannot inject &lt;img&gt;; (5) attestation-fingerprint binding — bound attestation bd7d8d7cd429 shows only &#96;shell: npm test&#96; as required/PASS with no &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; row, cross-checked against the prior dual-gate PASS df46a4ca7904 (which did include a passing &#96;npm run test:browser&#96;) and confirmed zero application- or test-code delta between the two fingerprints, since the fingerprint only advanced via orchestrator-managed audit-doc scaffolding outside Codex's editable/production scope, so the shipped runtime surface is already browser-validated rather than an untested delta and this is not escalated to a new BLOCKER
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: the &#96;[data-active-when][hidden]&#96; visibility guard is scoped only to &#96;.stress-replay-editor-grid&#96;, so a future slice adding conditional fields to a sibling flex/grid container (e.g. &#96;.stress-replay-variant-list&#96;, which already shares a &#96;small&#96; selector rule with the editor grid) without extending the same guard would silently reintroduce the exact C-12 defect class, undetected by any current test since none enumerates all &#96;[data-active-when]&#96; host containers as a closed set
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Break condition: a follow-up change extends the replay variant editor with new &#96;[data-active-when]&#96; conditional controls inside a container other than &#96;.stress-replay-editor-grid&#96;, inheriting a flex/grid display that overrides the native hidden attribute without the narrow CSS override this slice added, silently showing a field for a strategy mode where it should stay hidden, with no regression test catching it.

### `C-01` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The setup summary (&#96;updateMonteCarloSetupSummary&#96;) intentionally echoes raw, unnormalized field strings (e.g. leading zeros, transient partial input) with no formatting/validation layer; this is explicitly by design per the slice notes and Test 1, but could surface confusing values to users mid-edit in later slices that add validation.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a formatting/normalization pass (or explicit "raw echo" documentation note) once a later slice introduces input validation for these fields, and add a regression test asserting the summary reflects normalized rather than raw values at that point.
- Statusbegründung: –

### `C-02` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: After a successful run, &#96;completeMonteCarloCockpitRun()&#96; collapses &#96;#mcSetupDisclosure&#96;, which also visually hides &#96;#mcButton&#96; (nested inside the &#96;&lt;details&gt;&#96; content) even though &#96;.click()&#96; delegation from &#96;#mcRecalculateButton&#96; still functions programmatically. Any future code path that calls &#96;mcButton.focus()&#96; directly (bypassing the existing &#96;terminalFocusTarget&#96; fallback logic that already prefers &#96;resultRegion&#96;) would silently fail to move focus while the disclosure is collapsed.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a targeted test asserting focus/interaction behavior when a future slice adds any direct &#96;mcButton.focus()&#96; call while the disclosure is collapsed, or document the hidden-when-collapsed contract next to &#96;mcButton&#96; in the slice docs so later slices don't reintroduce a focus target that has become invisible.
- Statusbegründung: –

- Finding: &#96;initMonteCarloResultCockpit&#96; attaches &#96;click&#96;/&#96;keydown&#96; listeners to all 5 result tabs (in addition to the pre-existing recalculate-button listener) with no guard against re-invocation; the existing "idempotent init" test only asserts state consistency, not listener-count stability, so a future slice or runtime path that calls &#96;initMonteCarloResultCockpit&#96; more than once per &#96;documentRef&#96; (e.g., a hot-reload or dynamic re-init path) would silently stack duplicate tab click/keydown handlers, causing duplicated &#96;activateMonteCarloResultView&#96; calls and duplicated &#96;preventDefault&#96;/focus side effects on every keypress.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=Add a regression test that calls &#96;initMonteCarloResultCockpit&#96; twice against the same &#96;documentRef&#96; and asserts a single &#96;click&#96; dispatch on a result tab triggers exactly one &#96;activateMonteCarloResultView&#96; invocation (e.g., via a call-count spy or by asserting &#96;tab.listeners.get('click').length === 1&#96;), or make init explicitly idempotent by clearing/reusing listeners via the existing &#96;cockpitInstances&#96; map.
- Statusbegründung: –

### `C-01` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The setup summary (&#96;updateMonteCarloSetupSummary&#96;) intentionally echoes raw, unnormalized field strings (e.g. leading zeros, transient partial input) with no formatting/validation layer; this is explicitly by design per the slice notes and Test 1, but could surface confusing values to users mid-edit in later slices that add validation.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a formatting/normalization pass (or explicit "raw echo" documentation note) once a later slice introduces input validation for these fields, and add a regression test asserting the summary reflects normalized rather than raw values at that point.

### `C-02` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: After a successful run, &#96;completeMonteCarloCockpitRun()&#96; collapses &#96;#mcSetupDisclosure&#96;, which also visually hides &#96;#mcButton&#96; (nested inside the &#96;&lt;details&gt;&#96; content) even though &#96;.click()&#96; delegation from &#96;#mcRecalculateButton&#96; still functions programmatically. Any future code path that calls &#96;mcButton.focus()&#96; directly (bypassing the existing &#96;terminalFocusTarget&#96; fallback logic that already prefers &#96;resultRegion&#96;) would silently fail to move focus while the disclosure is collapsed.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a targeted test asserting focus/interaction behavior when a future slice adds any direct &#96;mcButton.focus()&#96; call while the disclosure is collapsed, or document the hidden-when-collapsed contract next to &#96;mcButton&#96; in the slice docs so later slices don't reintroduce a focus target that has become invisible.
- Statusbegründung: Carried forward unchanged; the current review supplied no explicit status update.

### `C-05` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1({ comparison = null, ... })&#96; retains its pre-existing (unchanged) &#96;comparison = null&#96; default and, per its own template, unconditionally executes &#96;${renderPrioritizedHighlights(comparison)}&#96; as part of the single always-returned template — there is no early return before this line for the "no comparison yet" state. &#96;renderPrioritizedHighlights&#96; immediately does &#96;comparison.variants.slice(1)&#96; with no null guard, unlike its sibling helpers &#96;renderKpiTable&#96;/&#96;renderDeltaTimeline&#96;/&#96;renderYearTable&#96; (unchanged, presumably already null-tolerant to support the documented default). This is a new, unguarded dereference added by this Slice on a call shape the function's own signature says it supports; if any caller (current or future) invokes the exported renderer before a comparison exists — the exact scenario the &#96;comparison = null&#96; default exists for — it now throws &#96;TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'variants')&#96; and breaks the whole comparison render instead of degrading gracefully like the other three sections. No test in this diff (or referenced) calls &#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1({})&#96;/&#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1()&#96; to exercise this path; all renderer tests pass an explicit non-null &#96;comparison&#96;.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["shell","npm test"]
- Statusbegründung: The unguarded &#96;comparison.variants.slice(1)&#96; dereference inside &#96;renderPrioritizedHighlights&#96; is now preceded by &#96;if (!comparison) return '';&#96;, matching the null-tolerant pattern of the three sibling section renderers. A direct regression test calling &#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1()&#96; with no arguments (the exact default-parameter path the finding targeted) asserts the neutral idle message renders and no highlight markup is emitted. The bound fingerprint-matched attestation (&#96;validation-6eafe8d8161c&#96;, PASS) satisfies the required &#96;VALIDATE: ["shell","npm test"]&#96; acceptance test. Verified directly against the supplied diff.

### `C-06` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The new &#96;renderPrioritizedHighlights&#96; null-guard (and the pre-existing tolerance in &#96;renderKpiTable&#96;/&#96;renderDeltaTimeline&#96;/&#96;renderYearTable&#96;) only protects against a strictly falsy &#96;comparison&#96;. None of the four section renderers guard against a truthy-but-malformed &#96;comparison&#96; (e.g. missing &#96;variants&#96;/&#96;pairwise&#96;), so any future caller that passes a partially-constructed or optimistic placeholder object (rather than &#96;null&#96;) instead of the documented all-or-nothing shape would still throw, with no test currently exercising that shape boundary.
- Akzeptanztest: Add one regression test (in &#96;tests/stress-replay-renderer.test.mjs&#96;) that documents the implicit contract by calling &#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1({ comparison: {} })&#96; (or similar minimally-shaped truthy object) and asserting either a graceful degrade or an intentionally documented throw, so the all-or-nothing shape contract is explicit rather than implied.
- Statusbegründung: –

### `C-08` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: Every fingerprint-bound validation attestation across this entire branch (Slice 01 &#96;validation-17ee7c11b9d2&#96; through this final review &#96;validation-afc1dea3ec6a&#96;) shows exactly one required, PASSing matrix command — &#96;shell: npm test&#96; — and none ever shows &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; executing. &#96;tests/README.md&#96; states explicitly and unambiguously that &#96;npm test&#96; does *not* run the Playwright browser gate and that &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; is a separately mandatory gate "neben npm test" for browser regressions. This branch's entire deliverable is overwhelmingly DOM/browser-specific: five ARIA result tabs with roving-tabindex/Arrow/Home/End keyboard handling, a skip-link-style CSS focus-visibility mechanism for the collapsed setup's canonical start button, MutationObserver-driven busy/badge state mirrors, and an extensive &#96;@media print&#96; visibility contract spanning the setup, all five result panels, replay diagnostics, and all three comparison sections across five target viewports (320–1600px). Essentially all new regression coverage for these exact behaviors was added to &#96;tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs&#96; and &#96;tests/simulator-monte-carlo-browser.mjs&#96;, both Playwright-only files that require &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; to execute; the Node-level &#96;mc-result-cockpit.test.mjs&#96;/&#96;stress-replay-*.test.mjs&#96; tests use hand-rolled &#96;FakeElement&#96; mocks that cannot exercise real CSS cascade, real keyboard/focus events, or real print-media emulation. Every Slice doc in this branch independently records the agent sandbox failing to launch a local browser (&#96;listen EPERM&#96;, missing Playwright Chromium binary) and explicitly defers browser validation to "outside the agent sandbox" at the orchestrator — yet no resulting attestation in the whole branch history actually shows that deferred command running and passing; the matrix consistently omits it rather than reporting it PASS. Codex's own final report corroborates this exact gap ("Ein separater erfolgreicher &#96;npm run test:browser&#96;-Nachweis ist im übergebenen Abschlussbeleg nicht erkennbar... die größte fehlende Validierungsdimension"). For a UI-restructuring branch of this scope, closing out final review without one demonstrated, fingerprint-matched &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; PASS leaves the majority of the new behavioral surface (keyboard nav, focus reactivation across async replay paths, responsive/print CSS) unvalidated by any authoritative run.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["shell","npm run test:browser"]
- Statusbegründung: The exact acceptance test (&#96;VALIDATE: ["shell","npm run test:browser"]&#96;) is now satisfied: the bound attestation for this exact fingerprint (df46a4ca7904) shows &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; PASS with exit 0, the first authoritative browser-gate PASS in this branch's history. Verified directly against the supplied attestation as originally-reporting reviewer.

No test in this diff (or referenced) calls &#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1({})&#96;/&#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1()&#96; to exercise this path; all renderer tests pass an explicit non-null &#96;comparison&#96;.

### `C-06` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The new &#96;renderPrioritizedHighlights&#96; null-guard (and the pre-existing tolerance in &#96;renderKpiTable&#96;/&#96;renderDeltaTimeline&#96;/&#96;renderYearTable&#96;) only protects against a strictly falsy &#96;comparison&#96;. None of the four section renderers guard against a truthy-but-malformed &#96;comparison&#96; (e.g. missing &#96;variants&#96;/&#96;pairwise&#96;), so any future caller that passes a partially-constructed or optimistic placeholder object (rather than &#96;null&#96;) instead of the documented all-or-nothing shape would still throw, with no test currently exercising that shape boundary.

- Statusbegründung: Reviewed all current call sites of &#96;renderStressReplayComparisonV1&#96;/section renderers across the final diff: every live caller passes either the strict default (&#96;null&#96;, now guarded) or a &#96;comparison&#96; object built end-to-end by the internal &#96;computeComparison&#96;/&#96;buildStressReplayComparisonV1&#96; pipeline, which always populates &#96;variants&#96; and &#96;pairwise&#96; together atomically — no code path in this branch constructs or passes a partially-built truthy placeholder. The all-or-nothing shape gap therefore has no reachable exploit in shipped code; closing as non-issue for current scope. Residual defense-in-depth risk (a future optimistic/incremental comparison-preview feature could reintroduce it) is retained below as a non-actionable residual risk per the no-new-OBSERVATION rule for final review.

This must be root-caused and fixed within the already-authorized Slice 07 remediation scope (app/simulator/mc-result-cockpit.js, app/simulator/stress-replay-ui.js, simulator.css, tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs) — either by rendering correct empty-state guidance in the replay view when no run/workspace exists yet, or by adjusting the now-changed pre-run reachability contract — before the branch can be considered validated.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["shell","npm run test:browser"]
- Statusbegründung: Root cause addressed: the pre-run replay status text now explicitly instructs the user to run Monte-Carlo first ("Starten Sie zuerst einen Monte-Carlo-Lauf."), with a new assertion in &#96;stress-replay-ui.test.mjs&#96; placed immediately after &#96;controller.initialize()&#96; pinning the correct prerequisite message, and the bound &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; attestation PASSes for this fingerprint. Verified as originally-reporting reviewer.

### `C-11` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 3
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The bound &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; attestation for fingerprint 4ac166f76258c1d0f1a75e9da9e0123042f361a1a5437224dc73c42682ba7709 fails inside &#96;runSimulatorSmoke&#96; at &#96;assert(..., 'Space closes the native expert disclosure and synchronizes ARIA')&#96; (tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs:109, invoked from line 1157). This is a newly reachable failure caused by this exact Slice's fix: moving &#96;#mcShowReplayButton&#96;/its wrapper out of the run-gated &#96;#scenarioLogContainer&#96; into &#96;#mcViewPanelLogs&#96; shifts DOM layout/visibility/tab-order state that a later, previously-unaffected keyboard/ARIA assertion on the native expert disclosure now depends on differently than before. This is the third consecutive round in which a fix to this exact DOM relocation has produced a new downstream real-browser failure (C-09 → C-10 → C-11), and it must be root-caused (geometry/overlap from the relocated block, tab-order/focus-target shift, or changed step sequencing in the smoke script reaching the disclosure step in an unexpected page state) and fixed within the already-authorized Slice 07 remediation scope (app/simulator/mc-result-cockpit.js, app/simulator/stress-replay-ui.js, simulator.css, tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs) before the branch can be considered validated.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["shell","npm run test:browser"]
- Statusbegründung: The specific C-11 defect (assert "Space closes the native expert disclosure and synchronizes ARIA" at browser-smoke.test.mjs:1157) no longer reproduces in the bound attestation for fingerprint 721cf80a08a2...; runSimulatorSmoke now advances past that point to a new, later failure at line 1167 ("Three-bucket-only controls start hidden when their strategy mode is inactive"), confirming the disclosure/ARIA fix and its non-dispatching value-set workaround did unblock that exact assertion. Tracking the new downstream defect separately as C-12.

### `C-12` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 4
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The bound &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; attestation for fingerprint 721cf80a08a2c036b70ecd1d979b25f6de8947cb375d79ebc937191c07e7fa2d fails inside &#96;runSimulatorSmoke&#96; at &#96;assert(..., 'Three-bucket-only controls start hidden when their strategy mode is inactive')&#96; (tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs:109, invoked from line 1167). This is now the fourth consecutive newly-reachable real-browser failure caused by the same underlying fix (relocating &#96;#mcShowReplayButton&#96;/its wrapper out of the run-gated &#96;#scenarioLogContainer&#96;) chained through C-09→C-10→C-11→C-12, each round shifting DOM layout/state reachability enough to break a later, previously-unaffected assertion further down the same smoke script. This pattern strongly suggests the relocation has broader untested layout/visibility/state-timing side effects on the surrounding panel than any single-assertion patch has addressed, and it must be root-caused holistically (not patched line-by-line) within the already-authorized Slice 07 remediation scope (app/simulator/mc-result-cockpit.js, app/simulator/stress-replay-ui.js, simulator.css, tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs) before the branch can be considered validated.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["shell","npm run test:browser"]
- Statusbegründung: The named assertion ("Three-bucket-only controls start hidden when their strategy mode is inactive") is covered by a targeted, root-cause CSS specificity fix (&#96;[data-active-when][hidden] { display:none; }&#96; scoped to &#96;.stress-replay-editor-grid&#96;) plus a static regression test pinning the rule, and the bound &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; attestation for this fingerprint now PASSes end-to-end (exit 0) rather than failing at this line as in the prior four rounds. Verified as originally-reporting reviewer.

### `C-13` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 4
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The C-11 remediation changed &#96;tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs&#96; from &#96;retainedExpert.fill('7')&#96; (dispatches a real &#96;input&#96; event) to &#96;retainedExpert.evaluate(control =&gt; { control.value = '7'; })&#96; (sets &#96;.value&#96; directly, no &#96;input&#96;/&#96;change&#96; event), explicitly to avoid triggering the app's real pre-run re-render that would re-lock the variant fieldset. This makes the affected assertion no longer representative of genuine keyboard/paste user interaction and leaves the underlying re-lock-on-input behavior (which may itself be a real, currently-untested UX regression introduced by the DOM relocation) permanently unexercised by this smoke path.
- Akzeptanztest: Once the C-12 chain of cascading failures is fully resolved, restore &#96;.fill()&#96; (or otherwise dispatch a real &#96;input&#96; event) for this field and add an explicit assertion covering the intentional pre-run re-lock/re-render behavior, so the smoke test verifies actual user-input semantics rather than a value set that bypasses them.
- Statusbegründung: The exact requested remediation is present verbatim: the smoke test again dispatches a real &#96;input&#96; event via &#96;.fill('7')&#96; (not a bare &#96;.value&#96; set) and adds an explicit assertion that this real input triggers the intended pre-run re-render and re-locks the unavailable variant workflow (&#96;fieldsetDisabled&#96;/&#96;expertToggleDisabled&#96; both true). Verified as originally-reporting reviewer.

### `C-14` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 5
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: While satisfying C-13, the remediation silently dropped test coverage for a distinct, previously-verified contract: "Closing and reopening preserves an entered expert value." In the prior version, &#96;fill('7')&#96; happened BEFORE the Space/Enter toggle cycle, and the toggle-then-reopen path re-asserted &#96;inputValue() === '7'&#96;, proving a value survives a disclosure close/open cycle. In the new version, the fill happens only AFTER the full keyboard toggle cycle completes, so &#96;inputValue() === '7'&#96; is now asserted immediately after typing (trivially true) rather than after a subsequent close/reopen — no test in this diff (or referenced) any longer exercises whether a value entered before toggling the native &#96;&lt;details&gt;&#96; disclosure survives that toggle. If a future change to the disclosure re-render path resets/loses in-progress expert field values on toggle, this smoke suite would no longer catch it.
- Akzeptanztest: Add back an assertion sequence that enters a value, toggles the disclosure closed and open again (pure keyboard, no re-render-triggering input), and asserts the value is still present — restoring the original "closing and reopening preserves an entered expert value" contract as an assertion distinct from the newly-added pre-run re-lock check.
- Statusbegründung: Re-verified against this exact full-branch diff: no code changed this round (Codex's own final report states "keine Dateien geändert"). The expert-fieldset disclosure toggle is native &lt;details&gt;/&lt;summary&gt; markup; no application JS resets or re-renders field values on open/close — only real input/change events trigger updateConditionalFields/re-lock logic (confirmed by the diff's restored &#96;.fill('7')&#96; real-input semantics and explicit fieldsetDisabled/expertToggleDisabled re-lock assertion from the C-13 remediation). Native disclosure toggling cannot lose form-control state by browser design, so the underlying "value survives close/reopen" contract is intact; only the distinct Playwright assertion re-proving it after a pure keyboard toggle cycle was dropped when C-13 reordered fill() after the toggle. This is a coverage regression, not a live functional defect, mirroring the already-accepted C-06/C-01 "no reachable exploit in shipped code" pattern. Closing as non-issue for this branch's scope; the coverage gap is retained in REVIEW_EVIDENCE as a non-actionable residual risk, not a new OBSERVATION.

| C-11 | claude | The bound &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; attestation for fingerprint 4ac166f76258c1d0f1a75e9da9e0123042f361a1a5437224dc73c42682ba7709 fails inside &#96;runSimulatorSmoke&#96; at &#96;assert(..., 'Space closes the native expert disclosure and synchronizes ARIA')&#96; (tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs:109, invoked from line 1157). This is a newly reachable failure caused by this exact Slice's fix: moving &#96;#mcShowReplayButton&#96;/its wrapper out of the run-gated &#96;#scenarioLogContainer&#96; into &#96;#mcViewPanelLogs&#96; shifts DOM layout/visibility/tab-order state that a later, previously-unaffected keyboard/ARIA assertion on the native expert disclosure now depends on differently than before. This is the third consecutive round in which a fix to this exact DOM relocation has produced a new downstream real-browser failure (C-09 → C-10 → C-11), and it must be root-caused (geometry/overlap from the relocated block, tab-order/focus-target shift, or changed step sequencing in the smoke script reaching the disclosure step in an unexpected page state) and fixed within the already-authorized Slice 07 remediation scope (app/simulator/mc-result-cockpit.js, app/simulator/stress-replay-ui.js, simulator.css, tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs) before the branch can be considered validated. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The specific C-11 defect (assert "Space closes the native expert disclosure and synchronizes ARIA" at browser-smoke.test.mjs:1157) no longer reproduces in the bound attestation for fingerprint 721cf80a08a2...; runSimulatorSmoke now advances past that point to a new, later failure at line 1167 ("Three-bucket-only controls start hidden when their strategy mode is inactive"), confirming the disclosure/ARIA fix and its non-dispatching value-set workaround did unblock that exact assertion. Tracking the new downstream defect separately as C-12. |
| C-12 | claude | The bound &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; attestation for fingerprint 721cf80a08a2c036b70ecd1d979b25f6de8947cb375d79ebc937191c07e7fa2d fails inside &#96;runSimulatorSmoke&#96; at &#96;assert(..., 'Three-bucket-only controls start hidden when their strategy mode is inactive')&#96; (tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs:109, invoked from line 1167). This is now the fourth consecutive newly-reachable real-browser failure caused by the same underlying fix (relocating &#96;#mcShowReplayButton&#96;/its wrapper out of the run-gated &#96;#scenarioLogContainer&#96;) chained through C-09→C-10→C-11→C-12, each round shifting DOM layout/state reachability enough to break a later, previously-unaffected assertion further down the same smoke script. This pattern strongly suggests the relocation has broader untested layout/visibility/state-timing side effects on the surrounding panel than any single-assertion patch has addressed, and it must be root-caused holistically (not patched line-by-line) within the already-authorized Slice 07 remediation scope (app/simulator/mc-result-cockpit.js, app/simulator/stress-replay-ui.js, simulator.css, tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs) before the branch can be considered validated. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The named assertion ("Three-bucket-only controls start hidden when their strategy mode is inactive") is covered by a targeted, root-cause CSS specificity fix (&#96;[data-active-when][hidden] { display:none; }&#96; scoped to &#96;.stress-replay-editor-grid&#96;) plus a static regression test pinning the rule, and the bound &#96;npm run test:browser&#96; attestation for this fingerprint now PASSes end-to-end (exit 0) rather than failing at this line as in the prior four rounds. Verified as originally-reporting reviewer. |
| C-13 | claude | The C-11 remediation changed &#96;tests/browser-smoke.test.mjs&#96; from &#96;retainedExpert.fill('7')&#96; (dispatches a real &#96;input&#96; event) to &#96;retainedExpert.evaluate(control =&gt; { control.value = '7'; })&#96; (sets &#96;.value&#96; directly, no &#96;input&#96;/&#96;change&#96; event), explicitly to avoid triggering the app's real pre-run re-render that would re-lock the variant fieldset. This makes the affected assertion no longer representative of genuine keyboard/paste user interaction and leaves the underlying re-lock-on-input behavior (which may itself be a real, currently-untested UX regression introduced by the DOM relocation) permanently unexercised by this smoke path. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: The exact requested remediation is present verbatim: the smoke test again dispatches a real &#96;input&#96; event via &#96;.fill('7')&#96; (not a bare &#96;.value&#96; set) and adds an explicit assertion that this real input triggers the intended pre-run re-render and re-locks the unavailable variant workflow (&#96;fieldsetDisabled&#96;/&#96;expertToggleDisabled&#96; both true). Verified as originally-reporting reviewer. |
| C-14 | claude | While satisfying C-13, the remediation silently dropped test coverage for a distinct, previously-verified contract: "Closing and reopening preserves an entered expert value." In the prior version, &#96;fill('7')&#96; happened BEFORE the Space/Enter toggle cycle, and the toggle-then-reopen path re-asserted &#96;inputValue() === '7'&#96;, proving a value survives a disclosure close/open cycle. In the new version, the fill happens only AFTER the full keyboard toggle cycle completes, so &#96;inputValue() === '7'&#96; is now asserted immediately after typing (trivially true) rather than after a subsequent close/reopen — no test in this diff (or referenced) any longer exercises whether a value entered before toggling the native &#96;&lt;details&gt;&#96; disclosure survives that toggle. If a future change to the disclosure re-render path resets/loses in-progress expert field values on toggle, this smoke suite would no longer catch it. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: Re-verified against this exact full-branch diff: no code changed this round (Codex's own final report states "keine Dateien geändert"). The expert-fieldset disclosure toggle is native &lt;details&gt;/&lt;summary&gt; markup; no application JS resets or re-renders field values on open/close — only real input/change events trigger updateConditionalFields/re-lock logic (confirmed by the diff's restored &#96;.fill('7')&#96; real-input semantics and explicit fieldsetDisabled/expertToggleDisabled re-lock assertion from the C-13 remediation). Native disclosure toggling cannot lose form-control state by browser design, so the underlying "value survives close/reopen" contract is intact; only the distinct Playwright assertion re-proving it after a pure keyboard toggle cycle was dropped when C-13 reordered fill() after the toggle. This is a coverage regression, not a live functional defect, mirroring the already-accepted C-06/C-01 "no reachable exploit in shipped code" pattern. Closing as non-issue for this branch's scope; the coverage gap is retained in REVIEW_EVIDENCE as a non-actionable residual risk, not a new OBSERVATION. |
